Are we talking hours, days, or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't been timing it or keeping track like that. \n\nUm... it's hard to say if it's just for a few hours or if it stretches into a couple of days. I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. Well, I guess what brings me in is that I just can't keep my mind on one thing anymore. \n\nI work at a warehouse, and lately... it's like every little noise pulls my attention away. A beeper will go off, or a forklift alarm, or even just someone moving a pallet across the floor, and it completely pulls me off my count. By the time I look back at what I'm doing, I realize I've put the wrong numbers into the inventory. It's just getting really frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice this problem with your attention and concentration? Was it sudden, or did it develop gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for about a month now. I guess it happened pretty suddenly. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home. Our usual routine completely changed, and... yeah, that's really when I first started noticing that I couldn't keep my focus at work.\n\nDoctor: Besides the trouble concentrating, have you noticed any changes in your mood over this past month? For example, have you been feeling more anxious, worried, sad, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, my mood has definitely been a little different lately. Some days I feel almost... I don't know, too upbeat? Like I have so much drive that I feel like I can fix up the whole house and sort out everything at the warehouse all before dinner. \n\nBut then, if my husband questions one of my plans, even just a little bit... I end up snapping at him over something really small. It just sets me off. So yeah, I guess I have been getting pretty irritable alongside feeling so energized.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that upbeat energy and drive to fix things around the house, how long do those periods typically last? Are we talking hours, days, or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't been timing it or keeping track like that. \n\nUm... it's hard to say if it's just for a few hours or if it stretches into a couple of days. Since all this started about a month ago, it just feels like that energy is kind of... always popping up. I just haven't really paid attention to exactly how long one of those specific periods lasts from start to finish.\n\nDoctor: When you're not feeling that upbeat and energized, do you go back to feeling like your normal self, or does that energized feeling seem to be pretty much there all the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's hard to tell. I guess... I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

By the time I look back at what I'm doing, I realize I've put the wrong numbers into the inventory. It's just getting really frustrating to deal with.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice this problem with your attention and concentration? Was it sudden, or did it develop gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for about a month now. I guess it happened pretty suddenly. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home. Our usual routine completely changed, and... yeah, that's really when I first started noticing that I couldn't keep my focus at work.\n\nDoctor: Besides the trouble concentrating, have you noticed any changes in your mood over this past month? For example, have you been feeling more anxious, worried, sad, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, my mood has definitely been a little different lately. Some days I feel almost... I don't know, too upbeat? Like I have so much drive that I feel like I can fix up the whole house and sort out everything at the warehouse all before dinner. \n\nBut then, if my husband questions one of my plans, even just a little bit... I end up snapping at him over something really small. It just sets me off. So yeah, I guess I have been getting pretty irritable alongside feeling so energized.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that upbeat energy and drive to fix things around the house, how long do those periods typically last? Are we talking hours, days, or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't been timing it or keeping track like that. \n\nUm... it's hard to say if it's just for a few hours or if it stretches into a couple of days. Since all this started about a month ago, it just feels like that energy is kind of... always popping up. I just haven't really paid attention to exactly how long one of those specific periods lasts from start to finish.\n\nDoctor: When you're not feeling that upbeat and energized, do you go back to feeling like your normal self, or does that energized feeling seem to be pretty much there all the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's hard to tell. I guess... I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? For example, did you start any new medications, have any illnesses or infections, or change anything about your diet or sleep habits?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, did your concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling start within the first few days of that change, or did you notice them developing more gradually over the following weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, I really couldn't tell you exactly when it started. I didn't keep track of the days or anything. I just know it all kind of began around the time he stopped working, but I honestly don't know if it was right away or if it built up over a few weeks. I just can't remember the exact timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that wired, energized, and irritable, does it ever completely go away for several hours or more, leaving you feeling calm and back to your normal baseline mood—or does that wired feeling pretty much stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't really go away. Once I wake up with that wired feeling, it just kind of stays with me all day. I don't really get any breaks where I feel calm or back to normal. It's just a constant, buzzing sort of energy from morning until night.\n\nDoctor: Before your husband stopped working and your routine changed, did you notice any anxiety, nervousness, or worry building up in the days or weeks leading up to that change?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling particularly anxious or worried before he stopped working. Everything was pretty much normal up until then. I didn't notice any kind of nervousness building up or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I haven't been timing it or keeping track like that. \n\nUm... it's hard to say if it's just for a few hours or if it stretches into a couple of days. Since all this started about a month ago, it just feels like that energy is kind of... always popping up. I just haven't really paid attention to exactly how long one of those specific periods lasts from start to finish.\n\nDoctor: When you're not feeling that upbeat and energized, do you go back to feeling like your normal self, or does that energized feeling seem to be pretty much there all the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's hard to tell. I guess... I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? For example, did you start any new medications, have any illnesses or infections, or change anything about your diet or sleep habits?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, did your concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling start within the first few days of that change, or did you notice them developing more gradually over the following weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, I really couldn't tell you exactly when it started. I didn't keep track of the days or anything. I just know it all kind of began around the time he stopped working, but I honestly don't know if it was right away or if it built up over a few weeks. I just can't remember the exact timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that wired, energized, and irritable, does it ever completely go away for several hours or more, leaving you feeling calm and back to your normal baseline mood—or does that wired feeling pretty much stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't really go away. Once I wake up with that wired feeling, it just kind of stays with me all day. I don't really get any breaks where I feel calm or back to normal. It's just a constant, buzzing sort of energy from morning until night.\n\nDoctor: Before your husband stopped working and your routine changed, did you notice any anxiety, nervousness, or worry building up in the days or weeks leading up to that change?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling particularly anxious or worried before he stopped working. Everything was pretty much normal up until then. I didn't notice any kind of nervousness building up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over this past month—like needing less sleep than usual, or sleeping significantly more or less than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep has been pretty much the same as always. I mean, I haven't noticed needing any less sleep or sleeping more than usual. It's just been my normal routine as far as that goes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? For example, did you start any new medications, have any illnesses or infections, or change anything about your diet or sleep habits?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, did your concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling start within the first few days of that change, or did you notice them developing more gradually over the following weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, I really couldn't tell you exactly when it started. I didn't keep track of the days or anything. I just know it all kind of began around the time he stopped working, but I honestly don't know if it was right away or if it built up over a few weeks. I just can't remember the exact timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that wired, energized, and irritable, does it ever completely go away for several hours or more, leaving you feeling calm and back to your normal baseline mood—or does that wired feeling pretty much stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't really go away. Once I wake up with that wired feeling, it just kind of stays with me all day. I don't really get any breaks where I feel calm or back to normal. It's just a constant, buzzing sort of energy from morning until night.\n\nDoctor: Before your husband stopped working and your routine changed, did you notice any anxiety, nervousness, or worry building up in the days or weeks leading up to that change?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling particularly anxious or worried before he stopped working. Everything was pretty much normal up until then. I didn't notice any kind of nervousness building up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over this past month—like needing less sleep than usual, or sleeping significantly more or less than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep has been pretty much the same as always. I mean, I haven't noticed needing any less sleep or sleeping more than usual. It's just been my normal routine as far as that goes.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, do you remember if you started noticing these concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling within the first week or so after that happened, or was it more like a couple of weeks or longer before you really noticed these changes?\n\nPatient: It was pretty much right away. I'd say within that first week of him being home and our whole routine getting turned upside down. I just haven't really paid attention to exactly how long one of those specific periods lasts from start to finish.\n\nDoctor: When you're not feeling that upbeat and energized, do you go back to feeling like your normal self, or does that energized feeling seem to be pretty much there all the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's hard to tell. I guess... I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? For example, did you start any new medications, have any illnesses or infections, or change anything about your diet or sleep habits?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, did your concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling start within the first few days of that change, or did you notice them developing more gradually over the following weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, I really couldn't tell you exactly when it started. I didn't keep track of the days or anything. I just know it all kind of began around the time he stopped working, but I honestly don't know if it was right away or if it built up over a few weeks. I just can't remember the exact timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that wired, energized, and irritable, does it ever completely go away for several hours or more, leaving you feeling calm and back to your normal baseline mood—or does that wired feeling pretty much stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't really go away. Once I wake up with that wired feeling, it just kind of stays with me all day. I don't really get any breaks where I feel calm or back to normal. It's just a constant, buzzing sort of energy from morning until night.\n\nDoctor: Before your husband stopped working and your routine changed, did you notice any anxiety, nervousness, or worry building up in the days or weeks leading up to that change?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling particularly anxious or worried before he stopped working. Everything was pretty much normal up until then. I didn't notice any kind of nervousness building up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over this past month—like needing less sleep than usual, or sleeping significantly more or less than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep has been pretty much the same as always. I mean, I haven't noticed needing any less sleep or sleeping more than usual. It's just been my normal routine as far as that goes.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, do you remember if you started noticing these concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling within the first week or so after that happened, or was it more like a couple of weeks or longer before you really noticed these changes?\n\nPatient: It was pretty much right away. I'd say within that first week of him being home and our whole routine getting turned upside down. It's been going on for about a month now, ever since he stopped working outside the house.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the wired feeling and concentration problems started within that first week after your husband stopped working. Looking back now, do you think these symptoms would have happened anyway even if your husband hadn't stopped working, or do you feel they were directly triggered by that change in your routine?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. I mean, it's hard to say for sure if it would have happened anyway. All I know is that it started right around the time he stopped working and our routine changed. I couldn't tell you if one caused the other or if it was just a coincidence.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Was it sudden, or did it develop gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for about a month now. I guess it happened pretty suddenly. It started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home. Our usual routine completely changed, and... yeah, that's really when I first started noticing that I couldn't keep my focus at work.\n\nDoctor: Besides the trouble concentrating, have you noticed any changes in your mood over this past month? For example, have you been feeling more anxious, worried, sad, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Well, my mood has definitely been a little different lately. Some days I feel almost... I don't know, too upbeat? Like I have so much drive that I feel like I can fix up the whole house and sort out everything at the warehouse all before dinner. \n\nBut then, if my husband questions one of my plans, even just a little bit... I end up snapping at him over something really small. It just sets me off. So yeah, I guess I have been getting pretty irritable alongside feeling so energized.\n\nDoctor: When you feel that upbeat energy and drive to fix things around the house, how long do those periods typically last? Are we talking hours, days, or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't been timing it or keeping track like that. \n\nUm... it's hard to say if it's just for a few hours or if it stretches into a couple of days. Since all this started about a month ago, it just feels like that energy is kind of... always popping up. I just haven't really paid attention to exactly how long one of those specific periods lasts from start to finish.\n\nDoctor: When you're not feeling that upbeat and energized, do you go back to feeling like your normal self, or does that energized feeling seem to be pretty much there all the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. It's hard to tell. I guess... I haven't really felt like my normal self at all since this whole thing started a month ago. \n\nWhether that wired energy is there every single minute or not, I'm just not sure. Like I said, I haven't been keeping track. It all just kind of blurs together for me right now, so it's hard to say if I ever fully go back to normal in between. It just feels like things have been generally off this whole month.\n\nDoctor: In this past month since things started feeling off, have you had any days or stretches of time where you felt more down, sad, or low in mood—like a return to feeling more like your usual self before all this started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't really felt sad or down at all this past month. It's just been that weird, wired feeling or the irritability I mentioned. I definitely haven't felt like my usual self, but it hasn't been a low or depressed kind of feeling either. Just... off.\n\nDoctor: Around the time this all started a month ago, did anything else change in your life besides your husband stopping work? For example, did you start any new medications, have any illnesses or infections, or change anything about your diet or sleep habits?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I haven't started taking any new medications, and I haven't been sick or had any infections or anything like that. My diet is exactly the same as it's always been. \n\nThe only major thing that happened was my husband stopping work, like you mentioned. Other than that, everything else in my life was pretty much business as usual before all this started.\n\nDoctor: Since this started a month ago, have there been any clear days or periods where you felt noticeably calmer or less energized than other days, or has that wired, energized feeling been pretty consistent throughout the entire month?\n\nPatient: I mean, I can't really point to a specific day where I felt completely calm. It's just... it's been pretty constant. I guess maybe some days I'm slightly less wired than others, but it's not like I wake up and feel completely relaxed or anything. It's just been this steady, sort of buzzing feeling the whole month.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you had any stretches of time—even just a day or two—where you felt noticeably more depressed, down, or where your energy level dropped significantly below where it normally is?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying earlier, I really haven't. I haven't felt depressed or down at all. And my energy definitely hasn't dropped... it hasn't dipped below my normal level at any point. It's really just been that same wired, buzzing feeling the whole time. I haven't had any kind of crash or low days like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you experienced anything unusual like seeing, hearing, or sensing things that other people around you don't seem to notice, or have you had any beliefs or ideas that seem different from what you normally think?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't seen or heard anything that isn't there. And my thoughts are... well, they're just my normal thoughts, just maybe going a little faster. But I don't have any strange beliefs or anything out of the ordinary like that.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working and your routine changed, did your symptoms start right away within days, or did it take a week or two before you started noticing the concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I didn't exactly mark it on a calendar. I just know it all started around the time he stopped working, but whether it was a few days or a couple of weeks after... I couldn't say for certain. It just sort of happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any head injuries, fevers, or any physical health problems at all during this past month, even something minor that you might not have thought was important?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't bumped my head or had a fever or anything. Physically, I've been completely fine. There really haven't been any physical health issues at all this past month.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, did your concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling start within the first few days of that change, or did you notice them developing more gradually over the following weeks?\n\nPatient: Like I said before, I really couldn't tell you exactly when it started. I didn't keep track of the days or anything. I just know it all kind of began around the time he stopped working, but I honestly don't know if it was right away or if it built up over a few weeks. I just can't remember the exact timeline.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling that wired, energized, and irritable, does it ever completely go away for several hours or more, leaving you feeling calm and back to your normal baseline mood—or does that wired feeling pretty much stay with you throughout the day?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't really go away. Once I wake up with that wired feeling, it just kind of stays with me all day. I don't really get any breaks where I feel calm or back to normal. It's just a constant, buzzing sort of energy from morning until night.\n\nDoctor: Before your husband stopped working and your routine changed, did you notice any anxiety, nervousness, or worry building up in the days or weeks leading up to that change?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling particularly anxious or worried before he stopped working. Everything was pretty much normal up until then. I didn't notice any kind of nervousness building up or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over this past month—like needing less sleep than usual, or sleeping significantly more or less than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep has been pretty much the same as always. I mean, I haven't noticed needing any less sleep or sleeping more than usual. It's just been my normal routine as far as that goes.\n\nDoctor: When your husband first stopped working, do you remember if you started noticing these concentration problems and that wired, energized feeling within the first week or so after that happened, or was it more like a couple of weeks or longer before you really noticed these changes?\n\nPatient: It was pretty much right away. I'd say within that first week of him being home and our whole routine getting turned upside down. It's been going on for about a month now, ever since he stopped working outside the house.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the wired feeling and concentration problems started within that first week after your husband stopped working. Looking back now, do you think these symptoms would have happened anyway even if your husband hadn't stopped working, or do you feel they were directly triggered by that change in your routine?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. I mean, it's hard to say for sure if it would have happened anyway. All I know is that it started right around the time he stopped working and our routine changed. I couldn't tell you if one caused the other or if it was just a coincidence.\n\nDoctor: If your husband were to go back to working outside the home and your routine returned to normal, do you think this wired, energized feeling and concentration problems would go away, or do you think they would continue regardless?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, it's hard to guess what would happen if things went back to how they were. Maybe it would settle down once we had our old routine back, but... I'm just not sure. It's really hard to say if it would just stop or keep going.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
